Copy the finished `runs/` folders back (`cluster/sync_from_trillium.sh`).

### 1. CAS(8e,8o) states at the headline minimum of 400 shots

**Why.** Every CAS(8e,8o) state was run at the default `n_min = 50` per context and fold (config `"II-A data, safe, bound start"`, trial files `runs/H2O_dz8o_*/trials/step5__*_rho=0.1__shard1of1.csv`). None was run at 400, the setting of Table IV (the `ref3_minshots_*` and `ref4_b400_*` jobs cover the 17 states of the a priori study and four H4 snapshots; none has a `dz8o` case; the `cp_*` jobs neither). So the CAS states are not a hold-out of the headline setting, and on the other systems 400 shots raised the cost of II-A by 1.3 to 3.3 where a selection ends before the contexts hold 400 shots per fold, so the CAS costs and the selection shares (0.9 to 5.4% per iteration, 1.2e9 shots on the near-tied CISD state) may be underestimated for the headline setting. The draft says this now (Results, Conclusions, abstract, SI table note); a run would replace the caveat by numbers.

**Tier 1 (the fixed-state comparison, Table `tab:window`)**: four states (eq and str, CISD and ADAPT 10), 16 trials, `--rho 0.1` (as at 50 shots: exact identification of the near-ties costs 6e12 shots for II-0), the configs at 400 that exist in `step5_external_baselines.py` (checked: `CONFIGS` has them, `with_rho` accepts them):
- `cluster/jobs/cas400_iiA_H2O_dz8o_{eq,str}_{CISD,ADAPT10}.txt`: 4 files, one node each, 16 workers, `"II-A data, safe, bound start, min shots=400, diagnose"`. At 50 shots one II-A trial took about 13 h and 32 GB (eq CISD; classical time per selection 11.7 h on the near-tied state, 2.5 to 3.6 h on the others); 16 trials run in one wave. **Risk**: a trial that does not finish within one 24 h allocation restarts from scratch after a requeue; if the log shows a trial above about 20 h on the CISD states, stop and rerun with `--trials 8`.
- `cluster/jobs/cas400_rest_{eq,str}.txt`: 2 files, one node each, two lines of 8 workers: `"II-0, ... min shots=400, diagnose"`, `"M1 seq, ... min shots=400"`, `"M2 safe, ... min shots=400"` on CISD and ADAPT 10 (at 50 shots: II-0 about 2 h, M1 seq 3 h, M2 1 h per trial).
```bash
for n in cas400_iiA_H2O_dz8o_eq_CISD cas400_iiA_H2O_dz8o_eq_ADAPT10 cas400_iiA_H2O_dz8o_str_CISD cas400_iiA_H2O_dz8o_str_ADAPT10 cas400_rest_eq cas400_rest_str; do
  sbatch --parsable --job-name="$n" cluster/trillium_run.sbatch "cluster/jobs/$n.txt"; done
```
**Tier 2 (optional, the path along the exact ADAPT trajectory, Table `tab:window_path`)**: `cas400_path_{eq,str}_{a,b}.txt` = II-A at steps 5, 20 (a) and 30, 40 (b), 16 trials, about 3 to 4 h each at 50 shots.

**Labels and seeds.** The labels carry `, rho=0.1` and the 400-shot config name, so the 50-shot files are not touched; the seed is the default 5, so the random streams are those of the 50-shot runs (paired comparison). Each command merges its case into `runs/<case>/<case>_step5_external_baselines.csv`.

**After the runs (what to change).**
1. `python scripts/sanity_checks.py H2O_dz8o_eq_CISD H2O_dz8o_eq_ADAPT10 H2O_dz8o_str_CISD H2O_dz8o_str_ADAPT10` (complete trials, correct selections within rho, shot orderings).
2. `scripts/paper_a_referee_tables.py`, `tab_window`: the `methods` list reads the config labels `"..., rho=0.1"`; add the 400-shot labels (`"II-A data, safe, bound start, min shots=400, diagnose, rho=0.1"`, `"II-0, safe, bound start, min shots=400, diagnose, rho=0.1"`, `"M1 seq, safe, bound start, min shots=400, rho=0.1"`, `"M2 safe, bound start, min shots=400, rho=0.1"`) as a second block with an `n_min` column, then `python scripts/paper_a_referee_tables.py --only tab_window` and `scripts/paper_a_window_path_table.py` for tier 2.
3. Text: Results, last paragraph of the total-cost question (the CAS paragraph: the caveat, "3.2 to 4.2 times", "47 times", the 11.7 h, "0.9 to 5.4%"); abstract (drop "run with the default settings"); Conclusions ("the 16-qubit states at the default minimum of 50 shots only"); SI `s_window_path.tex`, the `tab:window` note in `paper_a_si_clean.py` (`CAPTIONS`), `s1_notation.tex` (settings row "CAS(8e,8o), fixed states": n_min 50 and 400), `s1_extended_methods.tex` (the sentence that the states were not rerun at 400); rebuild with `paper_a_make_si.py`, `paper_a_inline.py`, `paper_a_si_refs.py`.
4. What to look for: does II-A stay the cheapest at 400 (best other / II-A was 3.2 to 4.2 at 50 shots), does the share of the selection in the cost of the next iteration move (0.9 to 5.4% at 50), the classical time per selection, and whether every selection is still within rho of the best generator.

### 2. Coverage of the selection on states with a set gap (round-1 D5)

**Why.** The reviewer asked for a coverage and variance-ratio test of the intervals and of the cross-fit estimator on arms with known gaps; the real states have the gaps chemistry gave them, so the test rotates a real template state until its relative gap |g|(1)/|g|(2) has a chosen value (leader unchanged): the support, the Pauli variances and the rare outcomes stay those of the template.

**What is there (all new, 2026-10-26).**
- `scripts/coverage_gap_states.py`: writes the states as a fictitious trajectory `<geometry>_GAPS` (cases `<geometry>_GAPS_ADAPT<j>`, j = 0 the template, 1.. the targets) into `.cache/trajectory/` and the table `runs/coverage_gap/<geometry>_GAPS_states.csv` (target and achieved gap, rotor, angle, leader). Tested in WSL on H4 1.0 ADAPT3 (targets 0.5 to 0.005, all reached), H4 2.0 ADAPT2 and LiH 3.0 ADAPT3. It needs the template trajectory and its problem pickle in `.cache/trajectory/` (`H4_square_eq_side1p0_HF_adapt.npz`, `H4_square_stretch_side2p0_HF_adapt.npz`, `LiH_R3p0_HF_adapt.npz` and the `*_problem.pickle`; present locally, check on the cluster) and PySCF (it rebuilds the Hamiltonian).
- `cluster/jobs/coverage_gap.txt`: 3 lines, 64 workers each (192 in all), seed 13 (new: add it to the list of seeds in SI Sec. S7.2): H4 1.0 (8 states, 1000 trials), H4 2.0 (5 states, 1000 trials), LiH 3.0 (3 states, 300 trials), each with four configs: `"II-A data, safe, bound start, min shots=400, diagnose"`, `"II-0, safe, bound start, min shots=400, diagnose"`, `"II-A data, safe, bound start, min shots=50, diagnose"`, `"II-A data, safe, bound start, anytime, min shots=200, diagnose"`. The `diagnose` configs record `miscovered_rounds`, `best_eliminated` and the `miss_*` fields (estimated over true sd ratio, thinnest context, first missed round).
- `scripts/paper_a_coverage_table.py`, `tests/test_coverage_gap.py` (3 tests, pass).
- Smoke test (WSL, 8 trials, H4 1.0 at a 1% gap, seed 13): II-A at 400 shots 6.9e6 shots mean, II-0 1.3e7, II-A anytime at 200 shots 2.0e7, 30 s, 18 s and 35 s, no wrong selection, a missed interval in 12.5%, 25% and 0% of the trials.
```bash
sbatch --parsable --job-name=coverage_gap cluster/trillium_run.sbatch cluster/jobs/coverage_gap.txt
# afterwards, anywhere:
python scripts/paper_a_coverage_table.py --geometry H4_square_eq_side1p0_GAPS H4_square_stretch_side2p0_GAPS LiH_R3p0_GAPS --latex ../reports/generated/tab_coverage.tex
```
**Cost.** H4: about 4 s per trial for II-A at a 1% gap, 2 s for II-0, anytime about twice: roughly 2 h per H4 line on 64 workers. LiH is about two orders of magnitude slower per trial (12 qubits, up to 1e8 shots): 300 trials, three states, no 1% state; if the line does not finish, rerun it (`--resume`) or lower `--trials`.

**What to look for.** Wrong selections out of 1000 trials (upper bound 0.3% for none; the guarantee is 5%); the share of trials with a missed interval for the per-round intervals (expected high, the intervals hold for one look per round), zero for the anytime intervals; best arm eliminated (0 expected); the ratio of the estimated to the true sd of the learned estimator (`miss_min_sdratio`; the SI quotes 0.75 to 1.35 without a design: summarize it from these trials); at 50 shots the late-state failure (sd ratio far below one) should reappear on the LiH states.

**After the run (what to change).** SI: a new part `si_parts/s_coverage.tex` with `% BEGIN INLINE tab_coverage` (as `s_nmin_table.tex`), added to `SECTIONS` in `paper_a_make_si.py` after `tab:diag`; rewrite the paragraph in `s_checks.tex` that lists the coverage test among the checks that do not exist; add `paper_a_coverage_table.py`, `coverage_gap_states.py` and the new runs to Table `tab:repro` (`s_repro.tex`) and to `REGENERATE` in `scripts/make_archive.py`; seed 13 in SI S7.2. Main text: one sentence in the Methods or Discussion on the coverage result, and the sentence on what was not tested. Tests: 185 now.

## 2026-10-26: round-3 reviewer questions (`reports/review_round3_reviewer_questions.md`)

- **Q1 (selection only or end to end; why 1.2)**: Table IV is stated to count the shots of the selection only (RQ C); the abstract and the Conclusions give the end-to-end consequence (4 to 28% against independent elimination on H2O at 1e-5 and 1e-3, about 1% or less on H4, from the selection share of 0.1 to 14%); the 1.2 case is explained in RQ C: the two smallest ratios (1.24, 1.37, H4 CISD) are against the reuse baseline Ikh, which reads the last energy evaluation for free while II-A in Table IV does not; with the same free data given to II-A at the default minimum of 50 shots the ratios are 2.6 and 3.2 (II-A with reuse at 400 shots not run: the only open part of Q1 edit 4); wide gap, and short selections (4 to 6e4 shots) on which the 400-shot minimum costs 2.3 and 3.3.
- **Q2 (distance to the oracle)**: `scripts/paper_a_ladder_table.py` writes SI Table `tab:ladder` (S9) from the stored runs, `runs/paper_a/ladder.csv`: (a) the condition of Table III (pairwise, estimated radii, 50 shots), five fixed states, ratio to II-0: M2 1.5 to 4.3, M1 seq 1.35 to 17, II-0 1, II-A 0.43 to 0.65, II-A with exact covariances and exact radii 0.46 to 0.59; the learned design costs 1.1 to 1.3 times the oracle on H4 and LiH and covers 76 to 84% of the gap from II-0 (91 to 94% from the cheaper of M2 and M1 seq), and 101 to 110% on H2O (cheaper than the oracle); II-0 with exact radii costs 1.01 to 1.08 of II-0 with estimated radii, so exact radii alone do not help; (b) the headline condition (400 shots), 17 states of the classical-prior study: the oracle design (exact covariance of the state, radii from the data, `true state, static`) costs 0.69 to 1.32 of II-A, geometric mean 0.99, cheaper on 10 states (lowest on H2O 0.69, 0.81 and BeH2 0.82, 0.88), dearer on 7. Discussion sentence on the distance to the oracle and to the two-arm bound (radius 3.6 to 4.8 times). This replaces the old "75 to 79% of the saving" sentence of RQ B (its two references were different II-0 rows).

## 2026-10-26 (night): Fir status check; the stretched-H4 line of the coverage test crashed (cause found and fixed); no ETA surprises

- **State on Fir at 13:54 PDT:** `cas400_iiA_*_CISD_fir` 588439 (eq) and 588443 (str) RUNNING since 11:17 and 11:25, `..._ADAPT10_fir` 592120/592121 since 11:49, `coverage_gap_fir` 594102 since 12:08. The II-A step-5 logs print only when a configuration ends, so the CAS jobs show no trial counts yet (16 workers, one wave; trial CSVs are written at the end). Expected: str CISD and the two ADAPT10 states at 400 shots a few hours to under about 8 h (2.5-3.9 h per trial at 50 shots, 1.3-3.3x the shots at 400); eq CISD close to a day (13 h per trial at 50 shots): the 24 h limit may be hit and a trial restarted after a requeue starts from scratch; `fir_progress.sh` (new) shows the logs and finished trials.
- **Coverage on Fir:** the H4 1.0 line finished (8 states x 4 configs x 1000 trials, last output 12:36); the LiH line is at its second state (300 trials per config, about 12-28 min per config and state, so roughly 5-8 h in all); the **H4 2.0 line crashed at 12:08** with `ValueError: H4_square_stretch_side2p0_GAPS_ADAPT0: commutator and sector gradients differ by 5.71e-01`.
- **Cause.** `coverage_gap_states.py` built its system with `AdaptSystem.build` = a fresh SCF. For stretched square H4 a fresh SCF on Trillium and on Fir gives other orbital signs than the cached trajectory (built in WSL; the same difference of 0.5705 in the stored gradients at step 2 on both clusters, `cluster/tri_gap_diag.sh`, `fir_gap_diag.sh`); H4 1.0 and LiH agree to 1e-15 and 1e-12. The template state is the cached one (state difference 0.0), so the states written for the job were computed in the wrong basis: the first log line read "relative gap 0.2908, leader D 0,3 -> 5,6" where the cached Hamiltonian gives 0.1082, "D 0,1 -> 4,5". The same crash would have hit the pending Trillium job 2657542. The tests on the workstation did not show it because the WSL build is the one that made the cache. This is the known build dependence of the degenerate H4 orbitals (README "Trajectory cases"), now on a new file.
- **Fix.** `scripts/coverage_gap_states.py`: `system_from_cache` builds the AdaptSystem from the Hamiltonian terms stored in the cached problem pickle (`metadata["hamiltonian_terms"]`, same construction as `AdaptSystem.build`, no SCF) and asserts that it reproduces the stored gradients of the template to 1e-8. Checked in WSL on H4 2.0 ADAPT2, H4 1.0 ADAPT3 and LiH 3.0 ADAPT3 (all run; stretched template gap 0.1082). The H4 1.0 and LiH lines already run on Fir and the same lines on Trillium are unaffected in substance (the cached and fresh Hamiltonians are equal to 1e-12). New script uploaded to both clusters (md5 equal to the local file).
- **Resubmitted:** `coverage_gap_stretch.txt` (only the H4 2.0 line; made from `coverage_gap.txt`) on Fir as `coverage_gap_stretch_fir` 613039 (PENDING). Trillium's `coverage_gap` 2657542 is still pending and now has the fixed script. Seed 13 on both; compare the shared lines when both finish.
- **Remember in the SI/seed list:** the stretched-H4 coverage states are built from the cached Hamiltonian (note for `s_checks` / `s_repro`). Uncommitted.

## 2026-10-27: Figure 1 redrawn as `reports/figures/pipeline_v2` (not yet in `draft.tex`)

- The Figure 1 of the draft (`reports/figures/pipeline.pdf`) has no source in the repository and no script produces it (`fig_pipeline` of `paper_a_figures.py` is an older matplotlib version that the draft does not use). Audit against Methods and Algorithm 1: the elimination box showed the interval rule (Eq. elim), not the sign-aware contrast (Eq. safe) of Algorithm 1; the stop said "rho-good" without "certified" and had no third exit (R at floor, uncertified); learning was drawn as always on (no pilot round with fixed estimators, no refit factor 1.5, no n_min per fold, no held-out acceptance test, no fixed fallback = II-0); no first radius R_0 and no inputs/settings; the figure ended at "append, reoptimise" (no outer loop, no run stop, no cost accounting C_sel / C_opt, no statement that only the selection is sampled and the reoptimization is exact and modeled); the reuse of the last energy evaluation (Ikhtiarudin baseline, also given to II-0 and II-A in the comparison) was absent.
- New source `reports/figures/pipeline_v2.tex` (standalone TikZ, Latin Modern, 6 to 7 pt text, 5 pt subscripts; 6.98 x 3.87 in, the old one 6.98 x 2.87 in) with two variants from one switch: `pipeline_v2.pdf` (no reuse arrow) and `pipeline_v2_reuse.pdf` (dashed arrow from the reoptimization box into the pool of shots, charged to C_opt). Build: `bash reports/figures/build_pipeline_v2.sh` (pdflatex twice, scratch folder, PNG previews `pipeline_v2.png`, `pipeline_v2_reuse.png`).
- JCTC figure rules checked (researcher-resources.acs.org, 2026-10-27): lettering at least 4.5 pt in the final size, lines at least 0.5 pt, double column 300 to 504 pt wide, depth at most 660 pt with caption; the new figure is 503 pt wide, smallest text 6 pt (5 pt subscripts), lines 0.4 to 0.7 pt (meter symbols 0.4).
- Not done: swap in `draft.tex` (line 130 `figures/pipeline.pdf`, width 0.92 -> `\textwidth`), new caption, copy to `presentation/figures/pipeline.pdf` (slide 6). Proposed caption: "One iteration of ADAPT-VQE with the selection expanded. Contexts and circuits are fixed once per selection; shots, splits, radii and elimination are repeated in every round, and only the sampled outcomes enter a round (the first radius R_0 uses A alone). Splits are refitted only by the learned estimators (II-A) when the shots held have grown by 1.5, with at least n_min shots per fold, and a fit is kept only if it lowers the variance on the held-out fold; otherwise the fixed fragments (II-0) are used. The selection returns a certified generator when it stops with one candidate left or by Eq. (rhostop), and an uncertified one when R reaches the floor. Only the selection is sampled: the parameters are reoptimized exactly and the cost of that step, C_opt, is modeled; the run ends at chemical accuracy, when every |g_i| < 1e-6, or 10 iterations after exact ADAPT (15 for the injected errors). Dashed (second variant): in the reuse baselines the shots of the last energy evaluation enter the pool and are charged to C_opt."

- 2026-10-27 (later): `pipeline_v2` trimmed to short labels on request (figure now 6.98 x 3.56 in, was 3.87; same two variants, same build). Removed from the boxes: the allocation target and "earlier shots count", the definition of R_0, the gate conditions of the refit, the floor/certified wording, the run-stop thresholds beyond 1.6 mHa and |g_i| < 1e-6, the C_opt formula, "charged to C_opt". These now belong to the caption, which replaces the proposal above: "One iteration of ADAPT-VQE with the selection expanded. Contexts are fixed once per selection; in every round the shots (target eps = R/z, earlier shots count), the splits, the radii and the elimination are repeated, and only the sampled outcomes enter a round. The first radius R_0 = max_i sum_l |A_il| uses A alone. Splits are learned (II-A) only when gated: the shots held have grown by 1.5, every context holds at least n_min shots per fold, and the fit lowers the variance on the held-out fold; otherwise the fixed fragments (II-0) are used. A selection is certified when it stops with one candidate left or by the rho-good test, and uncertified when R reaches its floor. Only the selection is sampled: the parameters are reoptimized exactly and the cost of that step, C_opt, is modeled (Sec. on resource accounting); a run ends at chemical accuracy, when every |g_i| < 1e-6, or 10 iterations after exact ADAPT (15 for injected errors). Dashed (second variant): in the reuse baselines the shots of the last energy evaluation enter the pool, charged to C_opt."

The text says so, frames II-0 as the light choice, notes that the refit loops over independent generators and folds (parallelizable; run on one core, speed-up not measured, so no 10 to 50 times claimed).
- **Narrative** (abstract, Conclusions): elimination moves the bottleneck; sequential estimation without elimination makes the selection 18 to 82% of the modeled total on H2O (`tab_q7_baselines.tex`; the reviewer quoted 42 to 82%, which is the stretched geometry only), the static estimator needs 17 to 121 times the shots along trajectories, tied leaders do not terminate without a tolerance (1.7e9 shots in one trial).
- **"Certified"** (Methods, Introduction): defined as the stopping rule at its nominal per-round level, not a run-wide guarantee; empirical reliability reported (0 of 1,884; at most 0.4% in the coverage test); anytime intervals about twice the shots.
- `scripts/paper_a_minshots_headline.py --rho 0.1` reads the `, rho=0.1` runs of `rho01_headline_*` (matched comparison only; writes `*_rho0.1` files and `tab_minshots_rho0.1.tex`; unchanged output at rho = 0 verified by md5). Run it when the jobs are in.
- **Yen et al. reproduction: not done, deliberately.** Their Tables III and IV (arXiv 2201.01471, PDF text in the session tool-results) are variances of the Hamiltonian estimator for H2, LiH, BeH2, H2O, NH3 in STO-3G with the Bravyi-Kitaev mapping, all bond lengths 1 A (H2O 107.6 degrees), exact or CISD covariances; FC grouping SI -> ICS: LiH 0.882 -> 0.232, BeH2 1.11 -> 0.459 (exact) or 0.495 (CISD), H2O 7.59 -> 1.50 or 1.68, NH3 18.8 -> 3.32 or 3.42; their SI is sorted insertion by coefficient. Our code estimates gradient rows of a pool problem (Part I `GradientProblem`, Jordan-Wigner, first-fit contexts, other geometries); reproducing their numbers needs a Hamiltonian-as-target problem object, BK Hamiltonians and their geometries, and a sorted-insertion grouping; only the ratios ICS/SI (0.20 to 0.45) are comparable across mappings. It is a separate piece of work (about a day) and should be a session of its own; until then the draft claims no reproduction of their tables and says the Hamiltonian is not tested.
- Abstract after the edits: 183 words, four sentences (was 174).

## 2026-10-27 (night): REMAINING JOB for another session: reproduce the Hamiltonian-splitting tables of Yen et al. with our splitting code (round-3 Q3 edit 3, critique Q18)

**Why.** The draft says the learned splitting saves less (finite shots, selection) than the several-fold variance reductions reported for the Hamiltonian (Yen et al. 2023, Choi et al. 2022), and "we make no claim about the Hamiltonian" (RQ B, Discussion). A reproduction would (a) show that our splitting solver (II-A, Eq. gls) reproduces their reduction on their own target, so the smaller gain here is due to the selection setting and not to the code, (b) fill the testing gap (no independent published number checks the splitting; `tab:tests`, SI Sec. S4.1), (c) answer critique Q18 (baseline fidelity). The SI already reproduces Huang and Izmaylov (`tab:reproduction`, `scripts/reproduce_huang_izmaylov.py` is the template for a reproduction script and its CSVs/table).

**Target numbers** (arXiv:2201.01471v3, Tables I, III, IV; text saved in `<local file>`; or fetch the PDF again):
- Setup: qubit Hamiltonians in STO-3G with the **Bravyi-Kitaev** mapping; R(H-H) = 1 A (H2), R(Li-H) = 1 A, R(Be-H) = 1 A collinear, R(O-H) = 1 A with angle 107.6 degrees, R(N-H) = 1 A (NH3, see the footnote of their ref. 27 for the angle); "variance of the Hamiltonian estimator" for a normalized total budget (sum of m_alpha = 1) and optimal allocation; covariances from the exact ground state (Table I) or the CISD state (Tables III and IV), final variances evaluated with the exact state.
- Fully commuting groups, Table IV (CISD covariances): **SI -> ICS**: LiH 0.882 -> 0.232, BeH2 1.11 -> 0.495, H2O 7.59 -> 1.68, NH3 18.8 -> 3.42 (H2: 0.136 for all). With exact covariances (Table I, FC): LiH 0.232, BeH2 0.459, H2O 1.50, NH3 3.32. Ratios ICS / SI: LiH 0.26, BeH2 0.41 to 0.45, H2O 0.20 to 0.22, NH3 0.18. IMA (measurement allocation only): LiH 0.647, BeH2 1.02, H2O 5.89, NH3 13.7. LF (largest first): 1.43, 5.19, 43.4, 78.8.
- Qubit-wise commuting (Table III, CISD): SI -> ICS: LiH 2.09 -> 0.978, BeH2 6.34 -> 4.40, H2O 48.6 -> 13.8, NH3 97.0 -> 45.5. Optional: our splitting is for FC contexts (the QWC splitting has about N_q^3 more variables; `contexts.qwc_groups` exists).
- Their SI = sorted insertion by coefficient magnitude, non-overlapping groups, allocation optimal; ICS = coefficient splitting over the overlapping groups, optimized by their iterative scheme (ours: the convex quadratic, `design.FragmentProblem.optimise`).

**What our code offers** (all in `part2_estimator_design/`): `GradientProblem` (`../part1_full_reproducibility_package/src/gradients.py`) holds `commutator_terms: list[dict[Pauli label, coefficient]]`, one operator per "generator", so **the Hamiltonian can be a one-generator problem** with `commutator_terms = [H terms]` (no commutator needed), an `evaluator` for a state and `labels`/`n_qubits`; `contexts.build_context_library(problem, strategy)` builds the library from `problem.parent_fc_groups()` (first-fit in decreasing Pauli weight; **their SI sorts by coefficient magnitude**: `GradientProblem.set_fc_routine(ordering, seed)` switches the insertion order, check whether a coefficient order exists, else add one); `sampler.OracleMoments(library, state)` gives the covariances of a state; `design.build_fragment_problems(problem, library, moments, "II-0" | "II-A")` builds the fixed and the splitting design; `FragmentProblem.variance(shots, x)` and `.optimise(shots)` evaluate and minimize. With one operator the optimal allocation for fixed coefficients is m_alpha proportional to sqrt(V_alpha) (variance (sum sqrt V_alpha)^2 for budget one), and with splitting the allocation and the coefficients are optimized alternately (their loop; ours: the planning code in `allocation.py`, check `step3_oracle_ceiling.py` for a similar single-state computation).

**Plan.**
1. `scripts/reproduce_yen_splitting.py` (new): build the Hamiltonians (OpenFermion + PySCF, STO-3G, geometries above, **BK** via `openfermion.bravyi_kitaev`; also JW to show the mapping dependence), wrap each as a one-generator `GradientProblem` (see how `trajectory.py` / `cases_extra.py` build problems; `trajectory.py` stores `hamiltonian_terms` through `openfermion_qubitop_to_dict`), states: exact ground state (full CI on the qubit Hamiltonian, 12 to 16 qubits: sparse eigensolver) and CISD (`part1_bridge`/`trajectory` has the CISD builder).
2. For each molecule and covariance source (exact, CISD): variance for (i) LF-like first-fit FC grouping with optimal allocation (our II-0), (ii) sorted insertion (needs the coefficient-order grouping), (iii) splitting with covariances of the CISD state, evaluated on the exact state (II-A: fit on CISD covariances, apply with exact ones; `FragmentProblem.variance` with the exact moments). Report the same columns as their Table IV and the ratios ICS / SI.
3. Write `runs/paper_a/yen_reproduction_<tag>.csv` and a generated table `reports/generated/tab_yen.tex` (as `tab_reproduction`), a block in the SI (evidence block C6 next to the Huang-Izmaylov reproduction, `si_parts/b3_baselines.tex`, ledger row in `b0_ledger.tex`, script map `s_repro.tex`, `make_archive.py` REGENERATE line, README), and a test (`tests/`: small molecule, variance of II-A not above II-0, reconstruction of the Hamiltonian coefficients) so that `tab:tests` can list "reproduces a published reduction of the Hamiltonian variance". Update the test count in the draft (Methods, Settings; currently 192) and `s_checks.tex`/`s_repro.tex`.
4. Draft: one sentence in RQ B (replace "we make no claim about the estimation of the Hamiltonian" by the result: our splitting reproduces ... within x% of their ICS / SI ratios on ... , or states the discrepancy and its likely cause: mapping, geometry, their iterative scheme not converging to the optimum, 2022 vs 2023 versions) and the Discussion sentence on the distance to the literature.

**Pitfalls.** (1) Numbers are only comparable up to the mapping (BK vs JW changes the Pauli structure and the grouping), the geometry (1 A is far from equilibrium for H2O and BeH2) and the grouping heuristic; judge the **ratios** ICS / SI, not the absolute variances, and report both mappings. (2) Our solver minimizes the variance exactly for given covariances and shots; theirs (ICS) iterates, so ours can be lower. (3) NH3: 16 qubits, 3,609 Pauli products, FC splitting has about 6,100 variables (their Table II): fine for the dual solver. (4) The H2 row is 0.136 for every method: use it as the first check of units (Hartree squared, budget one). (5) Windows pickles of cached problems are numpy-2: run the heavy part in WSL (`<venv>/bin/python`). (6) Keep to the DEVLOG rules: record the page counts and test count after the SI rebuild (`paper_a_make_si.py`, `paper_a_inline.py`, `build_pair.sh`, `paper_a_si_refs.py`).

**Other open items, no job needed from this entry:** the three `rho01_headline_*`, `crit_crossfit`, `crit_gamma`, `crit_reuse400` jobs submitted by the author: after they finish, run `scripts/paper_a_minshots_headline.py --rho 0.1`, write the cross-fitting and gamma tables (SI block R, one sentence each in the Discussion), and replace "II-A with reuse at 400 shots was not run" in Results RQ C by the numbers of `crit_reuse400` (compare with `Ikh reuse, FC, safe, bound start, min shots=400`).

## 2026-10-29 (late): TO RUN (cluster): oracle ceiling of the selection cost (grouping x splitting x rho); script and five job files written, nothing submitted

Paths relative to `part2_estimator_design/`; submit as in the entries above (`sbatch --job-name=NAME cluster/trillium_run.sbatch cluster/jobs/NAME.txt` or `cluster/fir_submit.sh`; strip carriage returns after a Windows checkout). One process per line (no workers, no sampling, deterministic), every line has `--resume` (skipped when its json exists). All 98 job lines were run through the real argument parser of the script (dry run with the solver stubbed). Output: `runs/paper_a/oracle_ceiling/<case>_<tag>.json`.

**Question (author, grouping discussion).** Is sorted insertion by a coefficient norm the right way to group, and would grouping together with coefficient splitting be better? What is the optimum, how far is II-A from it, how does the tolerance rho = 0.1 change it, and would redoing the grouping once only the close competitors are left pay?

**Model (`scripts/paper_a_oracle_ceiling.py`).** At a fixed state, with the exact covariances (`OracleMoments`), the exact gradients (so the leader and every gap are known) and the z of the run (`confidence_z`, Bonferroni, delta 0.05), the stopping test of the method (`rules.rho_good_stop`) asks for the sign of the leader, `|g_l| > z sd(g_l)`, and for each competitor `|g_l| - (1-rho)|g_i| > z sd(s_l g_l - (1-rho) t_i g_i)`, `t_i = sign(g_i)`. Each is a linear estimator with row `d = s_l A_l - (1-rho) t_i A_i` scaled by its required precision, so that its variance must be at most one. For a fixed list of contexts, shots and splitting weights together are a convex programme; `design.DesignSet.solve` (saddle form, already in the repository) gives its optimum, which is a lower bound on the shots of any procedure that certifies these contrasts with the same z (an adaptive procedure cannot meet them with fewer shots in total). Designs compared: `II-0` (no splitting), `weight` (the default first fit by Pauli weight), `mass` (sorted insertion by the pooled mass `sum_i |A_il|`), `contrast` (sorted insertion by the oracle sensitivity `max_r d_rl^2 Var(P_l)` to the scaled rows) and `family` (the union of the contexts of the three groupings, a richer list, so its optimum is the ceiling over this family, not the global one). Contexts are completed to maximal commuting groups (strategy `mass` as in step 4), so splitting opportunities come from the completion, not only from the first-fit group. Reported per design: shots, number of contexts, `n_eff` (participation ratio of the optimal shots over contexts), `spread` (number of groups that hold 80% of the contrast sensitivity; fewer is better). Not modelled: the sequential price (union over rounds, pilot rounds, n_min), the order in which arms are eliminated (all contrasts are certified at once), the non-binding sign of unresolved competitors (`--both-signs` in the check job).

**Code (uncommitted).** `scripts/paper_a_oracle_ceiling.py` (new; options `--rho`, `--strategy`, `--both-signs`, `--designs`, `--regroup K`, `--skip-compare`, `--resume`, `--out`); `tests/test_oracle_ceiling.py` (4 tests, WSL: the two-qubit toy with the real library and unit covariances, a hand-computed single-row bound `(sum_c sqrt(sum_l d_l^2/eps^2))^2`, the contrast rows at rho = 0 and 0.1). The full test suite was not rerun (the new file only imports existing modules). `--regroup K`: stage 1 certifies every competitor except the K closest and its shots are kept (they are valid samples of every Pauli of their context); stage 2 adds the K closest, over the same contexts (shots only added) or over those plus the contexts of a grouping keyed to the stage-2 rows (old contexts keep their shots as a lower bound, `allocate_topup`); the quantity reported is the shots added in stage 2.

**Correction to the discussion above.** The two-qubit examples given in the chat first used a hand-written solver in which a Pauli could only join a group whose members it commutes with, and a "mass loses by 1.5x" example whose mass tie (ZZ and XI both 1.2) was broken by floating-point noise (0.8+0.3+0.1 = 1.2000000000000002). With the repository's contexts (maximal groups) that example gives the same cost for all groupings (about 2517 shots). The test uses a tie-free toy (g1 = {XI 0.5, IZ 1.0, XX 0.5}, g2 = {ZI 0.2, IZ 0.8, XX 0.1}, g3 = {XI -1, YY -0.5, ZZ -0.1}, eps 0.05 and 0.2): weight and mass give the same partition and 509 shots, the contrast key puts the critical pair XI, XX in one context and needs 349 (family 347); the penalty of the weight grouping stays (1.46 early, 1.49 once g3 is eliminated), because the near-tied contrast dominates both stages. Hand check of the single row: `(sqrt(164) + sqrt(32))^2 = 341` against 349 with both rows.

**Preliminary local result (one state, rho = 0, `H4_square_eq_side1p0_ADAPT3`, 42 rows, z = 3.102, about 5 min for the five designs):** II-0 14,787 shots; weight 6,094 (53 contexts); mass 13,933 (36); contrast 12,200 (35); family 4,892 (124). So on this state the default grouping is 2.0-2.3 times cheaper than both sorted-insertion keys and only 1.25 times above the family ceiling, and the keys with fewer contexts are worse: the opposite of the toy. Q1 (measured, II-A, 10 other states) found mass/weight between 0.40 and 1.04, i.e. the mass grouping usually cheaper; H4 ADAPT3 is not a Q1 state, and the oracle certifies all contrasts at once, so the two need not agree, but **the first thing to check after the run is the sign of mass/weight against `runs/paper_a/q1_grouping.csv` on the six shared states** (H4 eq CISD, H4 eq ADAPT5, H4 str ADAPT5, LiH 3.0 HF, LiH 3.0 ADAPT5, LiH 1.6 ADAPT3); if the oracle and the measurement disagree in sign, suspect the model (contrast-key construction, certify-all-at-once) before using the ceiling in the draft. The `--regroup` part of this state was still running when this was written.

| Job file | What it runs | Lines | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `oracle_ceiling_small` | 13 H4 and LiH headline states x rho {0, 0.1}, all five designs | 26 | about 1.5 GB each; minutes to an hour per line (H4 ADAPT3: 5 min) |
| `oracle_ceiling_check` | `--both-signs` on H4 eq CISD and LiH 3.0 HF (rho 0, 0.1), `--strategy canonical` on H4 eq CISD (rho 0, 0.1), designs weight, contrast, family | 6 | the ceiling should move by the solver tolerance only for `--both-signs`; the canonical completion shows how much of the splitting comes from the completion |
| `oracle_ceiling_regroup` | `--regroup 2 --skip-compare` on the 9 ADAPT states of H4 and LiH, rho 0 and 0.1 | 18 | the late re-grouping question |
| `oracle_ceiling_beh2`, `oracle_ceiling_h2o` | 4 BeH2 and 4 H2O states x rho {0, 0.1}, each split over three lines by design (tags a: II-0 + weight, b: mass + contrast, c: family) | 24 + 24 | 14 qubits; 7-10 GB each is an assumption (the family has about three times the contexts); the time per line is unknown, so these are optional until the small file has shown whether the ceiling is informative |

Order of value: `oracle_ceiling_small` and `oracle_ceiling_check` first, then `oracle_ceiling_regroup`; BeH2 and H2O only if the small states show a ceiling worth restating (family below the best single grouping by more than about 1.3x, or II-A far above the ceiling).

**After the run (not yet written).** `scripts/paper_a_oracle_tables.py`: merge the jsons (the a/b/c lines of one state), per state and rho the shots of every design relative to the ceiling, the measured II-A cost over the ceiling (ref3/ref4 for rho = 0, `rho01_headline` for rho = 0.1) and the effect of rho = 0.1 on the ceiling (expected, from 1/eps^2: the near-tie stops dominating); the Q1 cross-check; the regroup table (shots added in stage 2, same contexts against regrouped, and the number of extra contexts, which is the price in circuits, not in shots). Explain each table with the toy of `tests/test_oracle_ceiling.py` (`spread` = number of contexts the critical Paulis occupy; `n_eff` = how concentrated the optimal shots are). Decision rule for the paper: if the weight grouping is within about 1.3 of the family ceiling on the headline states, say so in one sentence (the grouping is not the bottleneck at the oracle level) and keep the Q1 table; if II-A is far above the ceiling, the gap is in the learning, not in the grouping.

## 2026-10-29 (late, addendum): oracle ceiling, local diagnostic on H4 eq ADAPT3 (why the sorted-insertion keys lose); read the `oracle_ceiling_*` results with this

State `H4_square_eq_side1p0_ADAPT3`, rho = 0 (42 rows, z = 3.102), local WSL run, scratch script outside the repository (the numbers below are the only record). Ceiling over the family of three groupings: 4,892 shots (124 contexts); rho = 0.1 lowers every design by about 31% and keeps the order (weight 4,188, mass 8,849, contrast 8,355, family 3,309). Late re-grouping (`--regroup 3`, rho = 0): stage 1 4,950; added in stage 2 with the same contexts 1,546, with 10 extra contexts 817; all at once 4,915.

| grouping | contexts | mean multiplicity of the 63 critical Paulis | II-A, exact covariance | II-A, diagonal covariance only | II-0, exact | II-0, diagonal | splitting gain (exact) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| weight | 53 | 2.13 | 6,094 | 4,911 | 14,787 | 7,475 | 2.43 |
| mass | 36 | 1.67 | 13,933 | 3,195 | 14,788 | 3,312 | 1.06 |
| contrast-keyed | 35 | 1.86 | 12,200 | 2,742 | 14,648 | 3,082 | 1.20 |
| family (union) | 124 | 5.65 | 4,892 | | | | |

- With independent Paulis (diagonal covariance, the model of the hand toys) the sorted-insertion keys win (contrast 1.8 times cheaper than weight). With the true covariances the order reverses (contrast 2.0 times dearer than weight): the exact cost of the sorted groupings is 4.4 times their diagonal cost, that of weight 1.24 times. Consistent with: sorted insertion packs the heavy Paulis into the same context, whose cross-covariances then add to the variance of the contrast, and the splitting that should repair it has few other contexts to move to (multiplicity 1.7-1.9 against 2.1; gain 1.06-1.20 against 2.43). The signs of the cross-terms were not inspected.
- The sign of the covariance against the sign of the contrast coefficient decides (Bell state: ZI and IZ always agree; the row ZI + IZ in one context has variance 4, the same 4/eps^2 shots as two separate contexts, against 2 in the independent model; the row ZI - IZ has variance 0). A key by coefficient size sees neither.
- Redundancy helps (the union), a single oracle-keyed partition does not. Next (not run): sorted insertion with a covariance-aware criterion (insert into the group that raises the estimated `(sum_c sqrt(v_c))^2` least), with the exact covariances first, then with the HF-prior and pilot-round covariances; and the union of the first-fit partition with appended contexts for the critical Paulis (`--regroup` measures a version of this). Q1 (measured, mass grouping often cheaper than weight) was not reproduced by the oracle on this state; the cross-check on the six shared states stays the first step after `oracle_ceiling_small`.

- Late re-grouping at rho = 0.1 (same state, K = 3): stage 1 3,782; added in stage 2 with the same contexts 610, with 10 extra contexts 75; all at once 3,367. Totals: regrouped 3,857, same contexts 4,392 (rho = 0: 5,767 and 6,496). One state only; it does not support the earlier guess that the tolerance shrinks the benefit of re-grouping. The cluster file `oracle_ceiling_regroup` (9 ADAPT states) decides.

## 2026-10-29 (night): covariance-aware insertion implemented and tested locally on H4 eq ADAPT3; job file `oracle_ceiling_vaware` for the other 13 states (TO RUN, cluster)

**Code (uncommitted).** `scripts/paper_a_oracle_ceiling.py`: `moment_matrices(library, state)` (mean and covariance of every library Pauli from the state vector, `<PQ> = <P psi|Q psi>`; matches `OracleMoments` to 1e-12), `commuting`, `variance_aware_groups(library, rows, cov)` and the designs `vaware` (covariances of the state), `vaware_hf` (covariances of the Hartree-Fock determinant, `states.hartree_fock_state`), `weight+vaware` (default partition plus the vaware one); `--designs` accepts them (the default five are unchanged, so the earlier job files are not affected). The insertion: Paulis by decreasing sensitivity `max_r d_rl^2 Var(P_l)`; a group costs `sqrt(V_g)`, `V_g = sum_r` variance of the row restricted to the group, cross-covariances included; a Pauli joins the commuting group with the smallest increase of `sqrt(V_g)` or opens a group (equal multipliers over the rows, a proxy of the multi-constraint optimum). `tests/test_oracle_ceiling.py`: 7 tests pass in WSL (the moments against `OracleMoments` for a random two-qubit state; the Bell state: ZI - IZ merges, ZI + IZ does not; partition validity).

**Result (H4 eq ADAPT3, 42 rows, z = 3.102; both rho in parallel, 530 s):**

| design | rho = 0 shots (contexts) | rho = 0.1 shots (contexts) |
|---|---|---|
| weight (the floor: what the workflow uses) | 6,094 (53) | 4,188 (53) |
| family (weight + mass + contrast partitions) | 4,892 (124) | 3,309 (125) |
| vaware (state covariances, partition alone) | 9,103 (47) | 6,267 (56) |
| vaware_hf (Hartree-Fock covariances) | **4,186 (125)** | **3,034 (139)** |
| weight + vaware (union) | 4,734 (100) | 3,276 (109) |

- The covariance-aware insertion alone (47 contexts) is better than the sorted-insertion keys of the first run (mass 13,933, contrast 12,200) but still 1.5 times above the default partition: a partition with few contexts does not pay, even with the covariances in the criterion. What pays is the number of contexts (redundancy): the three designs below 5,000 have 100-125 contexts. `vaware_hf` is the cheapest found, 31% (rho = 0) and 28% (rho = 0.1) below the floor, 14% and 8% below the former "ceiling" (so `family` is only the ceiling over its own three groupings, not a global one). Why the Hartree-Fock covariances give 125 groups was not examined.
- **Reading for the workflow (not yet worth adding, and what would decide):** (1) the numbers are oracle numbers: the solve uses the exact covariances of the state and the rows the exact gradients (leader and gaps known); only the grouping used a heuristic (HF) covariance. The learned II-A costs 1.1-1.3 times its exact-covariance counterpart on the same grouping (SI Table `ladder`), more with many contexts. (2) The oracle optimum uses about 9-12 effective contexts (`n_eff`), but the method measures every context in its pilot round and needs `n_min` = 400 shots per context and fold, so 125 contexts cost 2.4 times the pilot of 53 and the oracle total (about 4-6 thousand) is far below that floor; the real comparison therefore needs the learned pipeline (step 4/5 with the grouping as an option, as Q1 did with `apply_grouping`), not the oracle. (3) The partition depends on the rows, i.e. on the gradients of the current state, so it would have to be rebuilt at every ADAPT iteration (new circuits); a state-independent version is not tested. (4) One state. The next information is the 13-state file below (is the 28-31% general, and does it survive at LiH?), then an end-to-end learned run with a `vaware_hf` grouping on two or three states.

| Job file | What it runs | Lines | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `oracle_ceiling_vaware` | designs weight, family, vaware, vaware_hf, weight+vaware on the 13 H4 and LiH headline states, rho 0 and 0.1 | 26 | about 9 min per H4 state (two rho in parallel); LiH longer (4096-dimensional Pauli images, a few GB), time unknown; `--resume`; output `runs/paper_a/oracle_ceiling/<case>_<rho>_vaware.json` |

## 2026-10-30 (later): toy study of key-based insertion against the global optimum (3 qubits, exact oracle); scratch scripts only, nothing in the repository, no cluster job

Question (author): why can no key-based sorting be optimal, and how is the global optimum of grouping + splitting computed with a full oracle? Setting: 3 qubits (135 maximal commuting groups), support of 6 Paulis, 2 scaled contrast rows (variance of each must be at most one), Haar-random state, the repository's solver (`design.DesignSet.solve`, level II-A, exact covariances, `OracleMoments`). "Global" = the optimum over the library of all 135 maximal groups (convex for a fixed library; adding groups can only lower it); "best partition" = the best over every commuting partition of the support (= every first-fit ordering reaches every partition) x five completion rules (canonical, mass, three random seeds). Keys: weight (default), mass `sum_r |d_rl|`, sensitivity `max_r d_rl^2 Var(P_l)`, covariance-aware insertion (`paper_a_oracle_ceiling.variance_aware_groups`), all completed with strategy mass. Scripts (session scratchpad `9a205fca-.../scratchpad`, not in the repository): `toy3.py`, `toy4.py` (library, solver, partitions), `keys4.py` (291 instances over four seeds, outputs `keys4_*.json`), `replay4.py` (regenerates two instances from their seeds, optimum structure, column generation).

**Result (291 instances, cost / global optimum):** best partition median 1.000, max 1.026 (a partition plus completion reaches the global optimum to 2.6% in these toys, so the optimum is not outside the reach of first-fit; **this corrects the chat statement that the optimum is "not a partition"**); weight mean 1.067, max 2.91, within 5% in 75% of the instances; mass 1.031 / 1.555 / 82%; sensitivity 1.026 / 1.555 / 84%; covariance-aware 1.024 / 1.555 / 85%. Best key (ties counted) in 209 (weight), 242 (mass), 250 (sensitivity), 255 (covariance-aware) of 291: none wins everywhere. In 2-qubit versions every partition equals the optimum (the completion supplies all overlaps), so the effect needs 3 or more qubits.

**Pair of instances (the same machinery, opposite winners).**
(A) support IYX, XZZ, XZX, IIY, IIX, IIZ; rows r1 = -30 XZX - 3 IYX - 3 XZZ, r2 = 120 IIX + 120 IYX + 3 IIY + 3 IIZ. Global 17,009 shots, 91% of them in contexts holding IIX and IYX together (covariance -0.29, same sign coefficients, so the sum has less variance than independent Paulis). Weight order puts IIX with XZX and IYX with XZZ: 2.91 times; mass, sensitivity, covariance-aware and the best partition: 1.00.
(B) support IZZ, XZY, IZX, ZYI, YYY, IXX; rows r1 = 20 YYY - 60 IZX + 10 XZY - 40 IZZ, r2 = -30 XZY - 120 YYY - 3 ZYI + 6 IXX. Global 13,141 shots in 10 contexts (YYY split over 9 of them; the optimum puts YYY and XZY together, covariance -0.33, same-sign coefficients). Weight order gives the partition {XZY, YYY, IXX}, {IZX}, {IZZ}, {ZYI}: 1.00. Mass, sensitivity and covariance-aware pair YYY with IZX (the two largest coefficients, belonging to different rows, covariance -0.35, opposite-sign coefficients) and XZY with ZYI: 1.555 times; the covariance-aware insertion fails here for a reason not diagnosed (its proxy assumes one constraint and no splitting).
Mechanism (consistent with both): putting a and b in one context changes the variance of row r by 2 d_ra d_rb cov(a,b) against measuring them apart, so the benefit of the pair is `-sum_r d_ra d_rb cov_ab`, a function of the pair (coefficients of both, sign, covariance), not of either Pauli alone; a first-fit order can only express "who is tried first", which fixes a pair's fate through the commutation structure, not through this benefit.

**Global optimization with the oracle.** (1) Enumerate or generate the library of maximal groups; solve the convex programme (shots and splitting jointly): 135 groups are enough at 3 qubits. (2) Column generation by re-solving (add the group with the largest decrease) from a cover of the support: (A) reached the optimum at once (17,009), (B) 32,977 -> 21,508 -> 16,118 -> 13,170 -> 13,140 with four added groups (global 13,141). (3) For larger systems the pricing is by the multipliers: a new commuting set S improves the optimum iff `sum_r mu_rS^T C_S^{-1} mu_rS / lambda_r > 4` (derived by eliminating the shots of a context; **not verified numerically**; a null direction of C_S means a zero-variance estimate), a maximisation over commuting sets (NP-hard, as it contains max-clique), done exactly for small systems and by greedy insertion otherwise. Caveats: 3 qubits, 6-Pauli supports, two rows, random Haar states and random coefficients, so the 15-25% of instances with a key more than 5% off say how often a key fails here, not how often on the molecules; II-A only (no zero-sum auxiliaries); the rows assume the leader and gaps are known; the oracle numbers on H4 and LiH (`oracle_ceiling_*`) are the real-system counterpart.

## 2026-11-10 / 11-11: classical covariance prior, work order CP-1.2 (`reports/prior/Classical_covariance_prior_work_order-2.tex`): implementation, rounds 1 and 2 (exploratory; draft not yet updated)

**Question.** Does a classical (sparse-state) or previous-iteration covariance prior reduce generator-selection shots, and must the energy discrepancy set its trust? Earlier selection-cost runs (floor-matched CPF, reframe GS-1.0, note arms; entries of 2026-11-05..10, `reports/prior`) found the prior neutral at matched floors; this work order separates design quality (Test A, true-variance evaluator) from selection cost (Test B, strict identification with the common Bernstein confidence of its Appendix A).

**Code (new).** `src/cp_order.py` (sources NEUTRAL / CLASSICAL / PREVIOUS, trust tau(x_+), design programs A1 / joint A2 / B), `src/cp_states.py` (the six states, round-2 states, degraded-prior siblings), `src/cp_testb.py`, `scripts/cp_order_run.py` (`calibrate`, `testa`, `testb`). Job files `cluster/jobs/cp_*.txt` (round 1), `cp2_*.txt` (round 2). Records: `runs/cp_order` (round 1), `runs/cp_lih` (in-family LiH calibration), `runs/cp_v2` (round 2). Deviations from the text: contexts are the repository's FC cover; states are archived trajectories (H2 regenerated); A1 per-arm programs use the exact stationary solve when its minimiser satisfies the L1 bound, CVXPY otherwise. **LiH joint programs (365,000 coefficients) cannot be solved by Clarabel within the 60 s cap: round 1 froze b^0 (600 of 600 programs); round 2 uses a block-coordinate descent (`CPSelector.alternating`: a feasible descent heuristic, NOT a certified optimum).**

**Cluster incident (Fir, 2026-11-10).** `cp_cal` (3324319) ran 4 h 19 without output and was cancelled: Clarabel starts a Rust thread pool of one thread per core in every process and ignores `OMP_NUM_THREADS` (193 threads per worker, load 690 on 192 cores; "Solution may be inaccurate" warnings only in that run). Fix: `RAYON_NUM_THREADS=1` in `cluster/fir_run.sbatch`, `cluster/trillium_run.sbatch`, `src/cp_order.py`, `scripts/cp_order_run.py`. The calibration was split into one task per (state, replicate, source, trust value) with a checkpoint (`calibration_units.jsonl`): 711 units, 5 min 26 s (job 3366568), 0 fallbacks in 31,029 design solves. Any job file with CVXPY/Clarabel needs the cap.

**Round 1 (work order as written: six states, 30 replicates, cap 1e7).**
- *Calibration (leave-one-family-out, 3 x 3 grid, constants 8/32/128):* every best setting on the grid edge; the best constant (128) beats the best energy rule in every fold (-0.086 vs -0.055 for the H2 fold); only the two LiH states respond to design (H2, H4 |log ratio| < 5e-4), so the H2 and H4 folds are fitted on LiH alone and the LiH fold on states where nothing moves (the work order says to label such a fold uncalibrated and pause; we ran it). x_+ = 0.06-0.09 (H2), 0.22-0.30 (H4), 0.62-0.66 (LiH): set by the Hoeffding energy radius r_E (3 Ha for LiH), not by the gap (H4 2.0 A has a 39 mHa gap and a smaller x_+ than H4 1.0 A, which has none). The frozen rule gives tau = 100-113 (H2), 19-31 (H4), **1.45-1.56 (LiH)**, a scale extrapolation: on LiH it keeps 8-70% of the benefit of constant 128.
- *Test A (worst arm, A1, log V_prior/V_neutral, LiH, constant 128):* N=0 -2.1, N=8 -0.8..-1.1, N=32 -0.13..-0.29, N=128 -0.015..-0.05 (top-gradient arm -0.05..-0.12 at N=128); exact covariance (TRUE) -0.020 / -0.037 worst arm at N=128. PREVIOUS can hurt: H2 1.5 A joint program +0.65 (N=0), +0.19 (N=32), because the HF history says many Paulis have zero variance. The H4 1.0 A joint-program TRUE reference was `optimal_inaccurate` in 30 of 30 programs and silently replaced by the frozen reconstruction (reference worse than neutral): defect, fixed in round 3.
- *Test B (cap 1e7):* certifies only H2 0.7414 and H4 2.0 A (priors 1.4-1.8x more median shots; mean ratio CLASSICAL 0.94 [0.81, 1.10], PREVIOUS 0.69 [0.60, 0.80]); H2 1.5 A (stationary, all gradients zero), H4 1.0 A, LiH 1.6 m=1 (exact tie 0.07384 / 0.07384) and LiH 3.0 m=3 unresolved at the cap; the LiH design program was skipped in every block, so all three sources ran the same b^0.
- *In-family LiH calibration (`runs/cp_lih`, job 3370428, 774 units, wide grid: d0 down to 1/2048, constants 1..2048 and the static prior; groups exact / degraded (half of the determinants: +14.6 mHa for LiH 1.6 m=1 with HF only, +31.4 mHa for LiH 3.0 m=3) / mixed):* constant trust is monotone for exact priors (-0.107 / -0.240 for tau >= 512, saturating) and peaks at 128 for the degraded ones (LiH 3.0 +31 mHa: -0.174 at 128, +0.014 for the static prior); constant 128 is within 0.003 (0.3%) of the per-state best trust on all four priors; the best energy rule has tau about 230 for every state (x_+ 0.616 vs 0.618, 0.658 vs 0.662 for exact vs degraded) and is within 0.002 of a constant.

**Round 2 (jobs 3430295-3430308, all COMPLETED; `runs/cp_v2`).** States LiH 1.6 m=2, LiH 3.0 m=3, 5, 6 (unique largest gradient; the sparse classical state is exact for m=2, 3 and slightly off for m=5, 6), degraded siblings (half of the determinants: +16.5 / +31.4 / +50.8 mHa), STO-3G water eq step 11 and stretched step 8 (water: Test A 30 replicates, Test B 10). Test A: trust sweep 8/32/128/512/static, A2 for four arms; Test B: cap 3e8, constant trust (classical 128, previous the full 128-outcome history).
- *Test A, exact priors:* A1 top-gradient arm at constant 128: -0.24..-0.41 at N=32, -0.045..-0.12 at N=128; worst arm -0.07..-0.34 at N=32, -0.01..-0.05 at N=128. Joint program (A2, alternating solver, 0 frozen): worst arm -0.05..-0.11 and top arm up to -0.23 (LiH 3.0 m=3) at N=128, i.e. 60-80% of what the exact covariance gives under the same solver. Trust 128 vs static differs by < 0.01.
- *Wrong priors:* static trust is harmful: LiH 3.0 +31 mHa worst arm +0.07 (N=32) and +0.32 (N=128); LiH 3.0 m=5 +51 mHa A1 +0.14 / +0.44, joint program with trust 128 +0.08 (N=32). Trust 8-32 never hurt in A1. x_+ = 0.651 (exact) vs 0.663 (51 mHa): the interval-based energy statistic cannot see these errors.
- *Test B (cap 3e8):* only LiH 3.0 m=3 certifies (neutral 26/30, classical 24/30, previous 23/30; correct 1.00; Wilson intervals overlap); R_N = 1.00 [0.96, 1.05] (classical), 1.04 [0.99, 1.10] (previous); degraded sibling 1.01 [0.96, 1.07]. LiH 1.6 m=2, LiH 3.0 m=5, m=6 and both water states are unresolved in all trials; ratios of consumed budget there are meaningless (a prior arm that stops earlier because the next checkpoint would exceed the cap has MORE survivors: water stretched R_N 1.12 / 1.18 with 81.7 / 83.7 vs 72.8 survivors); survivors at the cap show no progress advantage anywhere.
- *Reading:* the prior improves the design (more under the joint program, which keeps value to N=128), but under the common conservative confidence this has not turned into fewer shots in any state where Test B can measure it; trust should be moderate (32-128, not static), because a wrong prior costs more than a right one gains; the energy rule is not needed (no gain over a constant in-family; x_+ is blind at this energy precision). Candidate explanation for the missing translation, NOT tested: the range term of the Bernstein radius dominates.

**Critical review against the work order (still missing).** (1) exact-energy variant (evaluator-side, work order Sec. 4) not run; (2) the alternating solver is not validated against the conic program; (3) H4 1.0 A reference defect; (4) Test B informative on one state; (5) deliverables (four displays, calibration table, trust plot, cost accounting of energy and history shots, classical timings) not produced; (6) NEUTRAL acts as a regulariser, not as "no prior"; (7) the work order's pause limits (10 min, 2 CPU-hours) were exceeded without reporting first. Water is left out of round 3 by decision.

## 2026-11-11 (later): CP-1.2 round 3 submitted on Fir: exact-energy variant, alternating-solver validation, H4 1.0 A reference fix (water skipped; results to be appended)

**Code changes (checksums of Fir = local).** (1) `CPSelector.joint`: a conic solve that ends `optimal_inaccurate` is repeated at tolerance 1e-7, 1e-6, 1e-5 (1000 iterations); the tolerance reached is stored in `info["tol"]`. Cause of the H4 1.0 A defect: the exact-covariance reference (TRUE) was `optimal_inaccurate` at 1e-8 in 30 of 30 programs, `joint` returned no plan, and `_a2` fell back to the frozen b^0 (predicted worst-arm variance 0.00521, worse than the neutral design's 0.0039); at 1e-7 it is `optimal` and the alternating solver reaches 0.00398 after 6 sweeps (local diagnostic). Round-1 H4 1.0 A joint-program numbers (A2) and its TRUE row are therefore invalid. (2) `scripts/cp_solver_check.py` (new): alternating vs conic objective (A2 largest predicted variance at 128 shots per context; B total shots for e = 5e-3) and the trace after the frozen start and every sweep. First local result, H4 1.0 A, NEUTRAL, one replicate: after 2 sweeps (the round-2 setting) the alternating objective is +14.6% (A2) / +15.8% (B) above the conic optimum; after 6 sweeps +2.1% / +2.7%, still decreasing. The round-2 LiH joint-program numbers (A2, Test B designs) used 2 sweeps and are optimistic about convergence; they bias neutral and prior arms alike but are not lower bounds. (3) Exact-energy variant: `arm_inputs(..., {"exact": True})` uses x = |E_c - E_q| / |E_q| (evaluator side, never a selector input); `calibrate --exact-energy` (CLASSICAL source only), `testa --exact-energy --calibration ...` adds arm `CLASSICAL_XE` (tau from the frozen rule, group `loso_<case>`) next to NEUTRAL and `CLASSICAL_T128`.

**Jobs (Fir, 2026-11-11; whole nodes, `RAYON_NUM_THREADS=1` through the job script and the code).**
- `cp3_cal_xe` 3490996 (2 h): wide grid, exact x, four leave-one-system-out groups (`loso_LiH_R1p6_ADAPT2`, `..R3p0_ADAPT3`, `..R3p0_ADAPT5`, `..R3p0_ADAPT6`; the training states are the other systems and their half-determinant siblings), 3 replicates, about 1900 design solves; output `runs/cp_v3x`.
- `cp3_testa_xe` 3490997 and `cp3_testa_xeK` 3490998 (8 h, after 3490996): Test A, 30 replicates, held-out LiH 1.6 m=2, LiH 3.0 m=3, 5, 6 and the siblings; arms NEUTRAL, constant 128, energy rule with exact x; output `runs/cp_v3x`. Question: does an energy rule with exact energies beat constant 128 or the per-state best constant from the round-2 sweep?
- `cp3_h4` 3491062 (3 h): H4 1.0 A (m=1) and H4 2.0 A (m=3), Test A trust sweep (A2 with the retry fix) and Test B (cap 3e8, three sources, 30 replicates); output `runs/cp_v3`.
- `cp3_solver` 3491064 (12 h): `cp_solver_check` on all arms of H4 1.0 A, H4 2.0 A and H2 1.5 A (3 replicates, 12 sweeps), on the 6 arms of LiH 3.0 m=3, LiH 1.6 m=2, LiH 3.0 m=5 with the largest gradients (conic solves of at most 900 s), and convergence of the alternating solver alone on all 92 arms of LiH 3.0 m=3 and LiH 1.6 m=2 (10 sweeps, two replicates); output `runs/cp_solver`.

**Not covered.** Previous-state source in the exact-energy variant; water; the four displays and the calibration table of the work order; the round-2 A2 and Test B LiH designs are not rerun at more sweeps (decide after the solver validation).

## 2026-11-11 (afternoon): comparison with the extended LiH report; CP-1.2 Test B flaw (uniform allocation); note runs analysed; Test B rerun submitted

**Comparison with `Covariance_prior_extended_report.pdf` (CP-1.2 computational follow-up, 2026-11-10; LiH 3.0 A, m = 3, five replicates, one selection seed per method).** Test A agrees where comparable (neutral/prior worst-arm variance ratio, N = 32): constant trust 128: 1.38 (report) vs 1.33 (ours, 30 replicates); previous-state constant: 1.23 vs 1.23; energy rules 1.19 / 1.15 vs 1.07 / 1.11; the gap is the frozen trust (about 2.5 vs 1.45; the fitted scale s is 0.93 vs 0.61): the energy rule's parameters follow the calibration set's x_+ scale. Both find x_+ set by the energy radius (r_E = 3.07 Ha). Their single-seed selection trial (cap raised to 1e8, batch cap max(4096, 64 L_active)) gives neutral 20.9M shots, classical 14.6M, previous 11.6M (ratios 1.43, 1.80, no uncertainty; every joint fit fell back to fixed coefficients, so allocation only), ours R_N = 1.00 [0.96, 1.05] with neutral about 2.4e8-2.65e8 shots.

**Cause of the Test B disagreement: a flaw of our Test B on LiH.** The work order's first-checkpoint rule (at most 4096 shots) is below the sum of the floors (32 per context) as soon as more than 128 contexts are active (LiH: 933 x 32 = 29,856), so the loop in `cp_testb.run_cp` ends with every context at the floor: the allocation is uniform at every checkpoint (first block 955,392 = 933 x 32 x 32; later blocks 7,643,136 = 933 x 32 x 256), and only the coefficient splitting can differ between sources. LiH and water Test B of round 2 therefore do not test allocation. H4 (33 contexts) is unaffected. The statement "no shot saving where it can be measured" holds for H4 only. Fix: `--batch-fix` (cap max(4096, 64 L_active)).

**Note-protocol runs (`nt_*`, `nt2_*`, 21 states, 30 trials, Bernstein confidence; synced to the session scratchpad, `runs/note` on Fir; ratio = mean shots of NOTE_I / arm, geometric mean over states; NOTE_I = fixed reconstruction with measured-covariance allocation).** Allocation of the note protocol is not uniform (budget 2 L n_min per block).
- NOTE_II (prior for allocation only): **0.98**; NOTE_IV_DATA (splitting + allocation from measured covariance only, no prior): **1.54**; NOTE_IV (prior with updates, tau = 100): **1.57**; NOTE_III (static prior, splitting + allocation): **2.07**. So the covariance-informed splitting itself saves about 1.5x; an updated prior adds about +2% over data-only, a static prior about +35%.
- Per state, static vs data-only: BeH2 1.9x / 1.7x / 1.7x / 1.4x (ratios 3.30 / 1.70 etc.), H2O eq 5.49 vs 3.62, LiH 3.0 ADAPT3 2.13 vs 0.60, HF states 1.41 vs 0.66 and 1.86 vs 0.56; where the classical state is not accurate (H4 CISD) the static prior is 0.60-0.66 (worse than the baseline), the updated prior about 1.0-1.2. The headroom of a trust predictor is large in this protocol (static where the prior is right, updated where it is wrong), unlike in Test A at N <= 128: at selection scale most contexts hold few shots, so measured covariances are noisy and an exact static covariance keeps its value (consistent with the idea that contexts below the support floor never get a learned design; not tested directly).
- Gap-based trust arms (GABS / GREL, trust capped at 100): 1.47-1.57, no better than fixed tau = 100 (1.568); degraded priors (half of the determinants, 15 states): IV_HALF 1.68, gap-trust variants 1.55-1.60.
- Hoeffding confidence (Appendix A of the note): splitting hurts (static / updated priors 0.25x on H4 and LiH HF / ADAPT3 because the coefficient range enters the radius; BeH2, H2O and the large LiH states do not certify at all): the gain is a property of the Bernstein radius.
- Caveats: one protocol with my parameters (tau, eps_c = 0.1, gamma = 2, 6 alternations, n_min schedule), 30 trials per arm, ratios of means; the absolute costs (1e8-4e9 shots on LiH, BeH2, H2O) are 100-1000x the older II-A method; NOTE_II-IV were not paired with the CP-1.2 source definitions.

**Reading (supersedes the "no shot saving" statement of the CP-1.2 report).** Covariance-informed design saves shots under the Bernstein confidence; a static classical covariance adds to that where it is accurate and costs where it is not; an updated prior with tau of 100-128 (the CP-1.2 Test B arm) is about equal to data-only (1.02), which is what Test B showed on H4. The open question is therefore not "energy sets tau" but "can we tell when the classical covariance is accurate enough to be used static", and x_+ is blind at feasible energy precision. Decision pending: rerun Test B on LiH under the batch fix, including a static arm.

**Job.** `cp4_testb_fix` 3506125 (8 h): LiH 3.0 m = 3, LiH 1.6 m = 2, LiH 3.0 m = 5 (NEUTRAL, CLASSICAL tau = 128, PREVIOUS, CLASSICAL_INF static) and the 31 mHa sibling (CLASSICAL, CLASSICAL_INF), `--batch-fix`, cap 3e8, 30 replicates, 12 workers per line; output `runs/cp_v4`. All Fir partitions were reported down (`PartitionDown`) at 12:51 MST: jobs 3490997, 3490998, 3491062, 3491064 (round 3) and 3506125 are pending until Fir returns; `cp3_cal_xe` (3490996) completed in 24 min and has not been analysed.

**Rerun submitted (Trillium, 2026-11-11 18:05 EST):** `cp5_testb_sweeps` 2832625 (16 h limit): LiH Test B with the batch fix and 10 sweeps of the alternating solver (`--sweeps 10`; new `CPSelector.sweeps`, default 2), LiH 3.0 m=3, LiH 1.6 m=2 (NEUTRAL, CLASSICAL trust 128, CLASSICAL_INF) and the 31 mHa sibling (CLASSICAL, CLASSICAL_INF), 30 replicates, 24 workers per line, cap 3e8; output `runs/cp_v5`. LiH 3.0 m=5 and the previous-state source are left out (m=5 does not certify; previous shows no saving). Question: do the static-prior gains (1.19-1.26) survive converged designs? Expected duration several hours (about 5x more solver time per block than the 2-sweep run). The four Fir duplicates (3490998, 3491062, 3491064, 3506125) could not be cancelled (Fir connection expired).

**Check of the batch-fix runs (2026-11-11, night).** (1) Round 2 (literal 4096 rule): on LiH 3.0 m = 3, 63-65% of the blocks were exact multiples of 933 x 32 (uniform over all contexts), carrying 37-38% of the shots; the other blocks had fewer active contexts. The rule gives a uniform allocation whenever more than 128 contexts are active (not only in the first batches, not in every block). Reproduction of the first block with a real design (round 1 and round 6 epsilon): literal cap -> one distinct per-context count (32), batch fix -> 63-69 distinct counts, 69-89 of 933 contexts above the floor (the others stay at the floor of 32 and grow with the doubling: the preference survives only for the top 7-10% of the contexts at the first checkpoint). (2) Validity flags in the batch-fix runs are clean: block and history interval misses 0, maximizer eliminated 0, no interval conflicts, correct 29-30 of 29-30 certified. (3) **Design-program statuses in the batch-fix runs (LiH 3.0 m = 3 / LiH 1.6 m = 2, per arm):** the conic program is used when a block has fewer than 60,000 coefficients; there it ended `user_limit` (60 s), `error` (Clarabel SolverError) or `optimal_inaccurate` in many blocks, and the block then used the frozen reconstruction b^0: 11-14% (m = 3) and 33-39% (m = 2) of the blocks, similar across arms (neutral 215 of 639, classical 260 of 670, static 248 of 648 for m = 2) so no systematic bias, but the measured gain is a blend of program-designed and frozen blocks. Fix: `CPSelector.alternating_fallback` / `--alt-fallback` replaces a failed conic solve by the alternating solver. `cp5_testb_sweeps` was resubmitted with it (2832654; 2832625 had started at 18:09 with the old code and was cancelled).

**Round 6 submitted (Trillium, 2026-11-11 18:23 EST): later ADAPT steps and intermediate trust.** `cp6_testb_m56` 2832685 (20 h): LiH 3.0 m = 5 (overlap 0.998) and m = 6 (0.988) plus the 51 mHa sibling of m = 5, Test B with batch fix, alternating fallback, 8 sweeps, cap 4e9, 26 replicates, 13 workers per line; arms NEUTRAL, classical trust 128 / 1e3 / 1e4 / 1e5, static (siblings: trust 128 and static); output `runs/cp_v6`. `cp6_trust_cert` 2832686 (16 h): trust 1e3 / 1e4 / 1e5 on LiH 3.0 m = 3 and LiH 1.6 m = 2 (cap 3e8, 10 sweeps, 30 replicates; neutral, trust 128 and static from `cp5_testb_sweeps` 2832654, running since 18:13); output `runs/cp_v5`. Motivation: the note runs show the static advantage over data-only design shrinking along the trajectory (LiH 3.0: 3.5 at ADAPT3, 1.09 at ADAPT5; LiH 1.6: 1.94 at ADAPT2, 0.98 at ADAPT3 and 4), and no CP-1.2 selection ratio exists beyond m = 3. New source names: `CLASSICAL_T<value>` sets a constant trust. Uncertainty: m = 5, 6 may still not certify at 4e9; a per-trial time of several hours is expected (about 3 min per design solve at 10 sweeps).

## Report rewritten around the work-order deliverables, metric = percent change in shots (`reports/prior/CP12_results_report.tex/.pdf`, 10 pages)
- All round-1..8 records consolidated into `runs/` (copied from session scratchpads: `cp_order` incl. round-1 calibration, `cp_lih_cal`, `cp_v2`, `cp_v3`, `cp_v4`, `cp_solver`; synced from Trillium: `cp_v3x`, `cp_v5`-`cp_v8`).
- New `scripts/cp_report_tables.py` generates `reports/prior/cp12_tabs.tex` and `cp12_fig_{testa,success,error,trust}.pdf` from the records. Metric: Test B = 100 (mean shots arm / mean shots data-only - 1), paired bootstrap over replicate ids (10 000 resamples); Test A = same on the true worst-arm variance (variance ~ 1/shots at a fixed design). No log values in the report.
- Deliverables: displays 1-2 (Test A, Fig. 1, Tables 1-2), display 3 (certified-correct fraction vs shots, LiH, Fig. 2), display 4 (Test B summary with mean/median/90th pct, correct/wrong/unresolved, fallback designs, conflicts, Table 3-4), calibration table + trust plot (Table 5, Fig. 4), in-family calibration (Table 6), exact-energy variant (Table 7), cost accounting (Table 8, hand-collected from logs: contexts, history shots, classical time).
- New numbers from the consolidated analysis: static prior with a truncated state in Test B (10 sweeps, compared with the data-only design of the same quantum state): 2 determinants (31 mHa, LiH 3.0 m=3) +9.6% [-0.3, +20]; 3 determinants (51 mHa, m=5) -3.7% [-12, +5.2]. Trust 128 on the same: -1.4% [-12, +9.3], -5.8% [-12, -0.2]. Test A (A1, N=128) static: +37% and +55% (31 and 51 mHa), +1.1% at 16 mHa. Previous-state prior in Test B vs data-only of the same (2-sweep) designs: +7.6% [-1.6, +18] (m=2), -0.9% [-11, +11] (m=3). No wrong certified selection in any of the 2 524 Test B runs.

## Test B with the rho-good stopping rule (rho = 0.1), LiH and water submitted on Trillium
- Why: the main paper certifies a rho-good generator (|g_sel| >= (1 - rho) max|g|), rho = 0.1 on trajectories and rho = 0 on fixed-state benchmarks; near ties (several water states, exact ties at eq m=2, 4, 8, 12, 14) cannot be certified strictly and cost far more than the paper's trajectory setting.
- Code: `src/cp_testb.py` `run_cp(..., rho=0.0)`: the certificate is `lower magnitude of the leader >= (1 - rho) x upper magnitude of every other survivor` (interval form of Eq. (rhostop) of the draft, without its pairwise covariance term, so slightly more conservative); elimination stays exact; record fields `rho`, `exact_best` (selected one of the tied maximizers) and `correct` (rho-good: |g_sel| >= (1 - rho) top). `scripts/cp_order_run.py` gets `--rho`. At rho = 0 the behaviour is unchanged. Local smoke (H4, 4 replicates, 2 sweeps, static prior): rho = 0.1 certifies H4 1.0 A m=1 at 1.7e6-3.1e6 shots against 2.2e7-2.9e7 at rho = 0 (all correct); H4 2.0 A m=3 is unchanged (the top gradient is far ahead).
- Jobs (Trillium, `cluster/jobs/cp9_*.txt`, `--v2 --batch-fix --alt-fallback --sweeps 8 --rho 0.1`): LiH `cp9_testb_rho_lih_a` 2838738 (m=2, m=3 with arms data-only, trust 128, trust 1e5, static; 31 mHa truncated state of m=3 with trust 128 and static; cap 1e10, 30 replicates, output `runs/cp_v9`), `cp9_testb_rho_lih_b` 2838739 (m=5, m=6 with cap 4e10, 51 mHa truncated state of m=5; `runs/cp_v9`); water `cp9_testb_rho_H2O_{eq_m3,m7,m11,m16,str_m3,m8,m13,m17}` 2838740-2838747 (data-only, trust 128, static; 20 replicates, cap 1e10; `runs/cp_v10`; one node each, 60 workers at about 4 GB per worker from the water Test A memory, MaxRSS 120 GB for 30 workers).
- Not known: shots and wall time per water replicate at rho = 0.1 (no pilot; Test A needed 10 h per node for 30 replicates of 48 design solves each); cap 1e10 may be hit on some states.

## Correction: the measured energy record of the energy-rule arms is simulated and coarse
- The work order prescribes a simulated common energy record B_E = max(10^4, 32 L_E) shots with a Hoeffding radius when no optimization energy is stored; all measured-energy arms and the calibration used it (r_E = 3.07 Ha LiH, 0.29-0.41 H4, 20.7-20.9 water). It is far below the precision of a real VQE optimization energy and the radius uses the observable range, not its variance. By the 1/sqrt(B_E) scaling, r_E = 1.6 mHa on LiH would need about 3.7e10 shots with this bound (extrapolation, not a run).
- Consequence: the "x_+ cannot see mHa errors" result describes the 10^4-shot record only. The exact-energy variant (evaluator-supplied E_q) is the valid test of what energy information could do: on three informative LiH states constant 128 equals or beats the rule. An optimization-precision record was not tested.
- Report (`CP12_results_report.tex`), manuscript (abstract, Sec. II C, R7 paragraph, pending note), supplement (CP intro paragraph, Table S13 caption) and `REMAINING_TASKS.md` reworded accordingly; the x_+ derivation (worst-case r_E in numerator and denominator) is now written out in the report and the SI.

## Tier 0 check: energy rule with a small energy radius (lookup, no new runs; `scripts/cp_tier0_energy_radius.py`)
- Method: rule parameters (d0 = 0.00049, s = 0.004-0.034 by fold) from the leave-one-system-out exact-energy calibration (`runs/cp_v3x`); x_+ = (|E_c - E_q| + r_E)/(|E_q| - r_E) with the exact E_q; expected Test A (A1) ratio by log-linear interpolation of the constant-trust sweep (`runs/cp_v2`: trust 1, 8, 32, 128, 512).
- Validation: at r_E = 0 the lookup matches the measured exact-energy rule (`cp_v3x`) within 0.005 in R on all seven states.
- Result: r_E = 1.6 and 5 mHa leave trust and R essentially unchanged (accurate states trust 41-2000, R within 0.03; truncated 31 and 51 mHa states trust 1.4-8, R 1.00 and 0.99 against 1.07 and 1.03 for constant 128 at N = 32). r_E = 30 mHa discards the benefit on accurate states (m=3: trust 1.7, R 1.06 vs 1.36); the 3.07 Ha record gives trust 1 and R = 1.00 everywhere. So a minimal radius does not improve the rule: it is limited by its mapping, not by r_E. Limits: A1 only, estimate = truth, lookup not a run; A2 (joint program, where the exact rule was 0.64 vs 1.18 at 31 mHa) not covered.
- Hoeffding shots for these radii on LiH (1/sqrt scaling from 10^4 shots at 3.07 Ha): 5 mHa about 3.8e9, 1.6 mHa about 3.7e10.

## rho = 0.1 Test B results (LiH `cp_v9`, water `cp_v10`) and trajectory-level estimate
- LiH (30 replicates, 8 sweeps): trust 128 R = 1.19, 1.15, 0.97*, 0.97* (m = 2, 3, 5, 6); static 1.64, 1.49, 1.16, 1.06*; trust 1e5 1.63, 1.35, 1.14, 1.02*; truncated 31 mHa (m=3) trust 128 1.06*, static 1.27 [1.14, 1.40]; truncated 51 mHa (m=5) 1.02*, 1.03*. Data-only cost: m=2 1.59e8 (rho 0: 1.92e8), m=3 2.32e7 (1.89e7: higher despite the looser rho, 8 vs 10 sweeps), m=5 1.10e9 (1.61e9), m=6 6.87e9 (2.67e10).
- Water (20 replicates, cap 1e10): six of eight states certify. Trust 128: eq m3/7/11 1.11*, 1.01*, 0.91* [0.83, 1.00]; str m3/8/13 1.05*, 1.08*, 0.96*. Static: eq 1.51, 1.15, 1.04*; str 1.74, 1.40, 0.99*. eq m16 and str m17 unresolved in all arms (at most 2 of 20 certified).
- Findings: LiH picture unchanged; static gain falls along the water trajectory although the classical state stays accurate (error <= 2.2 mHa), so classical drift alone does not explain it (shots per selection grow at the same time); a wrong classical state no longer reverses the static ratio at rho = 0.1 (31 mHa: 1.27), the Test A harm (0.73, 0.65 at N = 128) is not seen at selection scale.
- Trajectory estimate (`scripts/cp_trajectory_estimate.py`, cumulative ratio over measured steps, bootstrap over replicates): LiH 3.0 steps 3, 5, 6: updated 0.99 (rho 0) / 0.97 (rho 0.1), static 1.09 / 1.08; water eq steps 3, 7, 11: 0.97 / 1.13; water str steps 3, 8, 13: 1.00 / 1.10. Last measured step carries 58-94% of the data-only total. End-to-end S_total = 1/((1-f)+f/s): s = 1.08-1.13 gives 1.004-1.016 at f = 5-14%; s = 1.74 gives 1.06 at f = 14% (f modeled in the earlier implementation, not measured).

### 2026-11-16 (night): review of the draft against the data; SG_FULL certificate asymmetry
- Finding: reframe.py certificate() gives the full-pool arm the strict-dominance test only at rho <= 0; at rho > 0 it must satisfy L_best >= (1 - rho) max U including the U of the leader itself.  SG_FULL (code FC_FULL) therefore costs more at rho 0.1 than at rho 0 on 14 of 25 matrix states (up to 67x, H4 1.0 CISD; BeH2 ADAPT 7 certified 100/100 strict and 3/100 at rho 0.1).  SE arms unaffected (rho 0.1 / strict 0.10 to 1.06).  All FC_FULL results at rho > 0 (matrix rho010 column, BeH2 trajectories rho005/010/020, rf3b rows, running job 2870577) are upper bounds on a fair full-pool cost; not yet fixed or rerun.

(11) includes the leader in the max (src/reframe.py `ceiling = max(mags[i][1] for i in survivors)`), so the rule needs the leader's own relative half-width <= rho/(2-rho) (5.3% at rho 0.1) while a sole survivor fires first for relative gaps above 2rho/(2-rho) (10.5%). Units: mHa -> m$E_h$ in the paper sources and figure labels (CSV and JSON column names unchanged).

- **Answers to the open items (2026-11-18, evening)**: K_m = m + 1 (author's rationale): the sparse state is a classical heuristic approximation of the quantum state for the design only, so the number of determinants grows linearly with the ansatz (text in II.C and SI S2).  Water geometries: STO-3G 0.9572 A / 104.52 deg = Benedict, Gailar and Plyler, J. Chem. Phys. 24, 1139 (1956) (checked through secondary citations; the paper itself was not opened); cc-pVDZ 0.9584 A / 104.45 deg is the archive geometry and the rounded value usually quoted, no primary reference found; best equilibrium 0.9578 A / 104.48 deg (Csaszar et al., J. Chem. Phys. 122, 214305, 2005).  CISD states exist only for H4 and water because Part I's case registry (part1_full_reproducibility_package/src/cases.py) defines HF and CISD only for them (LiH 3.0 HF only); LiH 1.6 and BeH2 CISD are defined in `src/cases_extra.py` but were never run; SI now says so instead of the author marker.

## 2026-11-19: JCTC stage-2 review, round 3 (wording)
- Paper: II.D now reconciles the 32-shot floor with II.E (every fragment of a block has at least 32 outcomes when its sample variance enters the block radius; the history bound's variance term equals its cap B^2/4 until n - 1 exceeds 8 log(2/zeta), about 200 outcomes) and defines the solver scales (smoothing = rel * sqrt(mean Q_alpha over loaded contexts) at the start of each inner minimization; lambda_i^(0) = (sum_alpha sqrt(q_i,alpha))^2 / (eps_r^4 * number of requested candidates) at the starting design x^0, `DesignSet.initial_multipliers`; n_alpha = sqrt(Q_alpha) recomputed at every multiplier step, `_Stack.dual_step`); 4096 called a protocol constant fixed before the runs; IV.B memory sentence says SG-SE 'does not optimize the splitting coefficients' (SG-SE does solve Eq. (9) at x = x^0); IV.F says BeH2 is the only *replicated* trajectory test of the prior and quotes the single cc-pVDZ ratio 0.91 (6.2e10 vs 6.8e10); III.C gives the tolerance of the sparse-state check (1e-12, 8-determinant example), delta = 0.05 and 20 candidates of the 4000-draw coverage test and the history-interval test (at most 5 misses in 2000 draws).
- Test: `tests/test_classical_prior.py::test_sparse_state_is_exact_when_nothing_is_truncated_and_obeys_the_cap` now asserts atol 1e-12, rtol 0 (was numpy's default allclose); passes.  On Windows 7 fixture-based tests of that file error because the cached problems were pickled with another numpy (`numpy._core`), unrelated; the full suite runs under WSL.
- Not changed (author decisions): the untied ADAPT snapshot indices have no stated selection rule beyond 'early and some later iterations'; marker A1 and funding/contributions remain.
